The exploit exploits how Jackson deserializes unknown JSON properties combined with how JavaScriptConfig validation works.\n\n### **KEY FILES AND LOCATIONS**\n\n#### **1. JavaScript Filter Implementation**\n- **File**: `/root/druid/processing/src/main/java/org/apache/druid/query/filter/JavaScriptDimFilter.java`\n  - **Lines 67-83**: `@JsonCreator` constructor that receives `JavaScriptConfig` via `@JacksonInject`\n  - **Lines 158-175**: `getPredicateFactory()` method that validates `config.isEnabled()` before compiling JavaScript\n  - **Lines 235-244**: `JavaScriptPredicateFactory` constructor that calls `cx.initStandardObjects()` which initializes the JavaScript scope with access to Java classes\n\n- **File**: `/root/druid/processing/src/main/java/org/apache/druid/segment/filter/JavaScriptFilter.java`\n  - Implements the actual filter execution using the predicate factory\n\n#### **2. The ObjectMapper is configured with `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES = false`\n   - This means Jackson silently ignores properties that don't map to any `@JsonProperty` fields\n   - The empty key `\"\"` in the JSON payload is an unknown property and gets silently ignored\n\n2. **Security Check Location**\n   - The vulnerability is in the timing of the security check\n   - In `JavaScriptDimFilter.getPredicateFactory()` (lines 158-175), the check `config.isEnabled()` is only performed when the filter is actually used (lazy initialization)\n   - The JSON deserialization itself doesn't fail even if JavaScript is disabled\n\n3. **Injected Configuration vs Deserialized Payload**\n   - `JavaScriptConfig` comes from `@JacksonInject`, not from the JSON being deserialized\n   - However, the vulnerability manifests through the fact that the filter object can be created and serialized even when JavaScript is disabled\n   - The malicious payload with empty key `\"\"` is designed to confuse analysis or bypass security filters that check the raw JSON\n\n4. **JavaScript Context Security Issue**\n   - Line 238 in `JavaScriptDimFilter`: `scope = cx.initStandardObjects();`\n   - `initStandardObjects()` creates a Rhino/Mozilla JavaScript context with standard objects including Java interop capabilities\n   - Once JavaScript is enabled, attackers can call Java methods like `java.lang.Runtime.getRuntime().exec()`\n\n---\n\n### **EXPLOIT FLOW**\n\n```\n1. Attacker sends JSON with JavaScript filter to Druid indexing endpoint\n2. Jackson deserializes the JSON (unknown property \"\" is silently ignored)\n3. JavaScriptDimFilter object is created with the malicious function\n4. If JavaScript is enabled in server config, getPredicateFactory() compiles the function\n5. The function runs in initStandardObjects() context with Java interop enabled\n6. Function calls java.lang.Runtime.getRuntime().exec() to execute system commands\n```\n\n---\n\n### **CRITICAL CODE SECTIONS**\n\n**Security Check (Line 162 of JavaScriptDimFilter.java):**\n```java\nPreconditions.checkState(config.isEnabled(), \"JavaScript is disabled\");\n```\n\n**Vulnerable Initialization (Lines 237-244 of JavaScriptDimFilter.java):**\n```java\nfinal Context cx = Context.enter();\ntry {\n  cx.setOptimizationLevel(9);\n  scope = cx.initStandardObjects();  // Creates scope with Java interop access\n  fnApply = cx.compileFunction(scope, script, \"script\", 1, null);\n}\nfinally {\n  Context.exit();\n}\n```\n\n**Jackson Unknown Properties Config (Line 53 of DefaultObjectMapper.java):**\n```java\nconfigure(DeserializationFeature.FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES, false);\n```\n\n---\n\n### **AFFECTED MODULES**\n\n1. **processing** - Core filter implementations\n2. **indexing-service** - Task and spec deserialization\n3. **server** - Configuration and module setup\n4. **core** - JavaScript configuration and parsing\n\nAll JavaScript-based components are vulnerable:\n- JavaScript filters (queries)\n- JavaScript aggregators\n- JavaScript post-aggregators\n- JavaScript extraction functions\n- JavaScript parse specs (ingestion)\n\n---\n\n### **ROOT CAUSE SUMMARY**\n\nThe vulnerability stems from:\n\n1. **Disabled property validation for unknown fields** - `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES = false` allows payloads with arbitrary extra properties including empty key `\"\"`\n2. **Lazy security validation** - JavaScript enablement is only checked at execution time, not at deserialization time\n3. **Rhino JavaScript context with Java interop** - `initStandardObjects()` creates a context where Java classes can be called from JavaScript code\n4. **No input sanitization** - The function code is compiled and executed directly without validation of its contents\n\n---\n\n### **TEST CASE DEMONSTRATING VULNERABILITY**\n\nLocated in `/root/druid/processing/src/test/java/org/apache/druid/query/filter/JavaScriptDimFilterTest.java`:\n- Line 102: Shows that `JavaScriptConfig(false)` should prevent JavaScript execution\n- Line 105: Exception message \"JavaScript is disabled\" is expected\n- The vulnerability bypasses this check through the empty key mechanism\n\nThis analysis provides a complete picture of how the vulnerability works and where the security controls are located in the Druid 0.20.0 codebase."},{"type":"text","text":"agentId: a0f0810 (for resuming to continue this agent's work if needed)"}]}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"16fe318a-1548-448b-8d34-f7f099d5babe","uuid":"2a7f251b-ded8-43a1-8efe-eaf078a80e28","tool_use_result":{"status":"completed","prompt":"I need to understand and fix a vulnerability in Apache Druid 0.20.0 located at /root/druid/. Looking at the code:\n\n1. `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES` is set to `false` in `DefaultObjectMapper.java`, allowing arbitrary extra keys to be silently ignored\n2. The empty key `\"\"` trick works because when Jackson deserializes JSON with polymorphic types using `@JsonTypeInfo`, extra properties with empty keys can potentially confuse the type resolution or inject arbitrary config values\n\nThe key issue is that the empty key `\"\": {\"enabled\": true}` can be used to override the injected `JavaScriptConfig` when Jackson deserializes the filter. This happens because Jackson may interpret the empty key as an attempt to set properties on the object being deserialized.\n\nThe fix should:\n1.
